---- dcb_conformance::append_is_atomic_under_a_mid_batch_fault stdout ----
SKIP append_is_atomic_under_a_mid_batch_fault: fixture declines `MID_BATCH_FAULT` — by scope, not by incapacity. This adapter supplies the reopen far end and not the fault far end, which is ES-35's live residual falsifier — a store that loses a write to a fault rather than to an instruction. The injection the testkit names IS available here: an AFTER INSERT trigger installed through a second connection, exercised by tests/append.rs::a_failure_mid_batch_leaves_nothing, where the append fails unabsorbed and rolls back every row. It is not armed from this fixture because ES-35's marker belongs to reopen-negative-control-and-durability-verdicts, and arming it here would retire that clause's falsifier from a story that has no say in it

SKIP rebuild_is_chunk_size_invariant: fixture declines `ProjectionProbe::READS_THROUGH_BATCH` — this adapter's batch exposes no read path, which PS-12 permits outright: `READS_THROUGH_BATCH` is `false`, so `probe_read_through` is never called and the rules that would have used it report this instead of asserting over a store that cannot answer them

---- projection_conformance::refused_reset_changes_nothing stdout ----
SKIP refused_reset_changes_nothing: fixture declines `RESET_REFUSAL` — SqliteProjectionStore holds no protection policy: the read model belongs to the caller and this adapter owns only the checkpoint row and the transaction that carries it, so there is no projection it could decline to reset and `reset` returns `Refused` on no path at all. The alternative — a protected-projections table invented here so that one more rule reports `Ran` — would put a domain policy into an adapter designed to keep the domain out, and certify a mechanism no application asked for

---- dcb_conformance::acknowledged_writes_survive_a_reopen stdout ----

---- dcb_conformance::append_reports_exceeded_store_limits stdout ----
SKIP append_reports_exceeded_store_limits: fixture declines `MAX_EVENT_DATA_LEN, MAX_TAGS_PER_EVENT, MAX_EVENTS_PER_BATCH` — this fixture states no ceiling for any store limit, so there is no capacity refusal for a rule to observe; a store with no ceiling is reporting a fact about itself rather than declining to co-operate

---- dcb_conformance::arming_a_mid_batch_fault_makes_the_append_fail stdout ----
SKIP arming_a_mid_batch_fault_makes_the_append_fail: fixture declines `MID_BATCH_FAULT` — this fixture cannot make its store fail between two rows of one batch; nothing in the port can reach inside an `append`, so the injection has to come from the adapter and this one has none to offer

---- dcb_conformance::recorded_time_survives_a_reopen stdout ----
SKIP recorded_time_survives_a_reopen: fixture declines `REOPEN` — GappyMemoryStore is a Vec behind an RwLock, so there is no durable medium to reopen over: discarding process state is indistinguishable from discarding the events

---- dcb_conformance::reopened_store_does_not_reissue_an_event_id stdout ----
SKIP reopened_store_does_not_reissue_an_event_id: fixture declines `REOPEN` — GappyMemoryStore is a Vec behind an RwLock, so there is no durable medium to reopen over: discarding process state is indistinguishable from discarding the events

---- dcb_conformance::arming_a_mid_batch_fault_makes_the_append_fail stdout ----
SKIP arming_a_mid_batch_fault_makes_the_append_fail: fixture declines `MID_BATCH_FAULT` — this fixture cannot make its store fail between two rows of one batch; nothing in the port can reach inside an `append`, so the injection has to come from the adapter and this one has none to offer

---- dcb_conformance::append_is_atomic_under_a_mid_batch_fault stdout ----

---- dcb_conformance::acknowledged_writes_survive_a_reopen stdout ----
SKIP acknowledged_writes_survive_a_reopen: fixture declines `REOPEN` — MemoryEventStore is a Vec behind an RwLock, so there is no durable medium to reopen over: discarding process state is indistinguishable from discarding the events

---- dcb_conformance::append_reports_exceeded_store_limits stdout ----
SKIP append_reports_exceeded_store_limits: fixture declines `MAX_EVENT_DATA_LEN, MAX_TAGS_PER_EVENT, MAX_EVENTS_PER_BATCH` — this fixture states no ceiling for any store limit, so there is no capacity refusal for a rule to observe; a store with no ceiling is reporting a fact about itself rather than declining to co-operate

---- dcb_conformance::recorded_time_survives_a_reopen stdout ----
